Attendance of meetings with 2 local HDR staff 
 
TASK SERIES 2.0 – Preliminary Design Phase 
 
Task 2.1 – Environmental Resource Review 
The following tasks indicate our team’s approach to preparing the project for federal funding by 
completing a resource review that could be used for National Environmental Policy Act (NEPA) 
documentation in the future. Due to the involvement of FEMA Funding, HDR assumes that a 
FEMA NEPA document will be needed as part of the project. FEMA will be the lead that 
determines the type of document, so proposed is a two-step process: 1) Environmental 
Resource Review, and 2) NEPA document. The Environmental Resource Review is included in 
this scope and step 2, NEPA document would be completed as an amendment. The steps are to 
determine the type of document needed as part of the Environmental Resource Review. HDR 
will coordinate with FEMA to request their determination, as we have done on other projects for 
communities, to make sure FEMA is involved from the beginning, which saves back tracking to 
meet their requirements.  
Desktop and field studies will be completed as part of the project in order to adequately evaluate 
resources within the Study Area. HDR will develop a study area that is 500-feet from the road 
centerlines for review and approval. This study area will be the basis for the resource review.  
The resource review will be summarized in an environmental overview report which will 
document findings for later use in NEPA and assist on selection of build concepts. Resources of 
note in the study area include: 
 

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
Project Name:  19th Street Drainage and Intersection Improvements Project 
 
Page 12 of 16 
 
Recreation Properties 
Section 4(f) or Section 6(f) properties in the study area will be identified and documented. No 
further coordination is anticipated for this task.  
 
Cultural Resources 
HDR will prepare a summary of existing conditions for cultural resources within the study area. By 
conducting a file search of the Wyoming State Historic Preservation Office (SHPO) WyoTrack 
database to identify known cultural resources within the file search area. The file search area will 
include the direct study area and a 1-mile radius. Cultural resources within the file search area 
will be identified and summarized. National Register of Historic Places eligibility status for each 
cultural resource will be presented and used to identify known or potential historic properties 
within the study area.  
Aquatic Resources   
Aquatic Resources will be field delineated using the latest US Army Corps of Engineers (USACE) 
methodology. Data from the aquatic resource report will be used for the resource review and the 
404 permitting in Task 3.2.  
Biological Resources  
A review of habitat for endangered and threatened species and other species of concern will be 
completed via windshield review of the study area. U.S. Fish and Wildlife’s Information for 
Planning and Consultation (IPaC) website will be used to generate a list of threatened and 
endangered species and evaluate using IPaC determination keys. A USFWS coordination letter 
will be prepared based on the species list to request comments. No species-specific survey are 
included in this scope of work. 
Socioeconomic Resources and Land Use 
A review of socioeconomic resources that could be affected and potential land use changes 
resulting from construction of the project will be identified for inclusion in the Categorical 
Exclusion and Environmental Commitment Checklist.   
Floodplains  
The project crossed the Crow Creek. Information from the hydraulics analysis and structure 
design will be included in the resource review to determine possible impacts and any future 
permit needs. 
Task 2.2 – Resource Overview Report 
The following elements will be included in an environmental overview report which will summarize the 
environmental resources for future use in NEPA. The repot will include: 
• A concise Purpose and Need  
• Alternatives considered.  
• Summary of resources reviewed in Task 2.1. 
 
Agency coordination will be prepared for agency review and comment to advise on the environmental 
concerns in the area, minimization measures, and possible future permitting.  
 

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
Project Name:  19th Street Drainage and Intersection Improvements Project 
 
Page 13 of 16 
 
Deliverables: 
• WY SHPO WYOTrack Record Search 
• IPaC generated species list 
• Aquatic Resources Delineation Report 
• Agency Coordination Letter and responses 
• Draft and Final Environmental Overview Report 
 
Environmental Resource Overview Assumptions 
o HDR assumes the summary of existing conditions will not include reviews of General 
Land Office (GLO) maps, historical maps and aerial imagery, Sanborn Fire Insurance 
maps, Laramie County assessor records, or other archival sources. 
o HDR assumes that the study area will not exceed 15 acres in size. 
o HDR will prepare draft agency letters as word documents and will send out the letters to 
the applicable agencies as determined necessary.  
o HDR assumes that the study will not require assessing potential effects to the Cheyenne 
West Historic District (48LA1418).  
o Ayres will provide any pertinent project or design information to HDR for completion of 
the ROW and Utility portions of the CE checklist as needed. 
o Assumes no standing structure relocations will be required for the project. Standing 
structure relocations may warrant an environmental assessment (a higher level of NEPA 
documentation), which would be handled with an amendment or separate contract.   
o Changes to the general project alignment may warrant an amendment to this scope of 
services. 
o This effort will not result in a NEPA decision. Once Federal funds are acquired and a 
lead federal agency has been identified, the class of NEPA documentation will be 
determined and completed under the direction of the federal agency.   
 
Task 2.3 – Structure Selection & Preliminary Design  
• 
HDR will conduct preliminary bridge design and develop design alternatives to determine cost-
effective bridge solutions as discussed in the RFP. 
• 
Two bridge alternatives (Steel and Prestressed Girder) may be developed to help evaluate 
structure type, size, and conceptual level cost estimate. 
o 
A structure comparison memo will be provided to document the selected structure type. 
• 
The City of Cheyenne has identified that the preferred structure will be a bridge type structure. 
o 
Specifically, it will be either a 2 or 3 span steel or prestressed girder bridge with a cast in 
place concrete deck. 
o 
The structure comparison memo will document and compare one potential alternate 
structure, such as buried structure, should it be applicable.  However it is assumed that a 
structure of this type will not be chosen based on input from the City. 
• 
Structure Deliverables include: 
o 
Roadway Alignment and Profile 
o 
Structure comparison memo and bridge Preliminary Layout plans 
o 
No design of retaining walls is anticipated during Preliminary Design phase 
 
 
Task 2.4 – Geotechnical Findings Evaluation 

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
Project Name:  19th Street Drainage and Intersection Improvements Project 
 
Page 14 of 16 
 
• 
Drilling and Lab Testing (the following has been provided for the Prime to provide to the Geotechnical 
Engineering Subconsultant) 
 
o 
The anticipated boring plan consists of two (2) geotechnical borings to be sampled to an 
approximate total depth of 50 feet below existing grade.  Final depth will be dictated by 
the conditions encountered; however, a minimum of ten (10) feet of rock coring will be 
required for termination.  The drilling and laboratory testing subcontractor will be 
expected to perform soil sampling utilizing the Standard Penetration Test (SPT) utilizing a 
140-pound hammer.  Soil sampling should be performed at 2.5-foot intervals to ten (10) 
feet below existing grade, followed by 5-foot intervals till auger refusal or total depth.  Soil 
samples shall be collected in glass jars and stored for future laboratory testing.  Rock 
cores shall be boxed in core boxes for future testing.  It is assumed no contaminated soils 
or groundwater will be present, and Level D PPE will be required.   
 
o 
Upon completion of the borings, groundwater levels shall be collected if encountered.  
Borings shall be backfilled to approximately 0.5 feet from the surface, with a patch in kind 
at the surface.  Drill cuttings may be used as backfill.   The contractor is responsible for 
maintaining the backfilled holes such that there is no settlement of the patch.   
 
o 
The drilling contractor is responsible for Maintenance of Traffic (MOT) at the site in 
accordance with WYDOT standards.  No time restrictions are anticipated.   
 
• 
Geotechnical Findings Evaluation 
 
o 
HDR understands that the Prime will subcontract a drilling firm to perform the 
geotechnical borings for the proposed structure.  HDR personnel will develop a 
recommended boring location plan with recommended testing, as needed based on site 
conditions.  Prior to mobilizing for the fieldwork, a meeting will be held to discuss the 
proposed sampling and testing requirements.  HDR personnel will coordinate with the 
subcontractor to be on site during the drilling operations, to log the samples, and evaluate 
the conditions of the site.  Upon completion of the borings, HDR will review the boring 
logs and provide a recommended laboratory testing program to the subcontractor.  
Laboratory testing may consist of USCS classifications, shear strength testing, 
unidirectional consolidation, and unconfined compressive strength of rock.  The actual 
laboratory testing program will be dictated by the conditions encountered in the borings.  
HDR will prepare geotechnical boring logs to be included in the foundation report.   
 
o 
HDR assumes the boring program will consist of two (2) borings at the project site, 
roughly in the opposite corners of the proposed structure, and will consist of Standard 
Penetration Testing (SPT) through the soil strata, followed by NQ-sized coring to depth.  
Assumed total depth of the borings is 50 feet, including ten (10) feet of rock coring.  
Maintenance of Traffic (MOT) will be handled by the subcontractor.  We assume no time 
restrictions for drilling operations, and therefore assume a total of three (3) days on site.

TASK SERIES 3.0 – Final Design Phase 
 
Task 3.1 – Structural Design (Bridge) 
• 
The selected bridge alternative will be designed according to the 9th Ed. of the AASHTO LRFD 
Bridge Design Specifications, 2020 and the Wyoming Bridge Design Manual. The design live load 
will be HL-93 
 
• 
Below is a list of anticipated bridge design items 
o 
Determine and refine the bridge geometry including length, width, and elevations. 
o 
Develop the bridge loading requirements, including the level of seismic analyses required 
o 
Design bridge superstructure including bridge girders and reinforced concrete deck 
o 
Design bridge crash barriers and any required bridge railing(s) 
o 
Evaluate need and design for bridge expansion joints and bearings 
o 
Design bridge substructure including abutments and piers and their foundations 
o 
Evaluate the bridge foundations for the scoured condition 
• 
Quality Assurance/Quality Control Review 
o 
HDR will review documents, specifications, assumptions, and calculations for accuracy 
and reasonableness. 
o 
QA/QC comments, responses, and resolutions for structure calculations, and special 
provisions will be documented and kept on file. 
o 
QA/QC Review will be completed by an experienced employee with HDR that has had 
limited involvement with the project and can review the project from a fresh perspective 
• 
Assumptions 
o 
The fee associated with this proposal is based on a bridge type structure being chosen 
for the replacement.   
• 
HDR will submit to the Wyoming Bridge Program per Wyoming statute 24-1-132 the following 
information: 
o 
To meet Wyoming Statute and federal requirements the following information is required 
to be submitted within ninety (90) days of the bridge being opened to traffic: 
 
Design calculations, plans, and specifications 
 
Load rating for all WYDOT Load Rating Trucks to include the AASHTO and 
WYDOT standard vehicles and the AASHTO EV2 and EV3 trucks following the 
Wyoming Bridge Design Manual and the AASHTO Manual for Bridge Evaluation 
3rd Ed  
 
Geotechnical report 
 
Hydraulic report.  The report will need to include scour elevations for the design, 
100 year, and 500-year events 
o 
All submittals will be stamped by a Wyoming registered professional engineer 
 
Task 3.2 – Geotechnical Recommendations (Structural) 
• 
Geotechnical Design Memorandum and Engineering Geology Sheets 
o 
HDR personnel will prepare a Final Geology Report, for structural related items, 
summarizing the geotechnical conditions at the site and will include foundation 
recommendations, construction considerations, and design limitations.  HDR will provide 
design recommendations per AASHTO design standards in the current LRFD Bridge 
Design Specifications and per WYDOT standards.  The report will include borings logs, 
laboratory testing results, design calculations and references, special provisions, if 
needed, and the Engineering Geology sheets.   The memo will be submitted 
electronically, and HDR assumes one (1) round of comments from the Owner.

Task 3.3 – 404 Permit Application 
• 
HDR will prepare a 404 Permit Application to the USACE evaluating overall project impacts to 
wetlands and/or waters of the US, potential cultural resources, and evaluate for threatened and 
endangered species. The following assumptions have been made in relation to this task:  
o 
All work required can be permitted under a Nationwide Permit.  
o 
No wetland mitigation will be required for the project. If wetland mitigation is deemed to 
be required, HDR can complete under additional scope.  
o 
No historic properties or archaeological sites are present within the study area.   
o 
No effect to any listed threatened or endangered species potentially present within the 
study area.   
o 
Habitat survey to confirm presence or absence of potential threatened and endangered 
species habitat will be conducted for the study area. Species specific or protocol surveys 
including fish surveys, or a Biological Assessment are not included in this scope, but if 
required may be negotiated as additional scope. 
o 
No additional permits for local or state agencies are included in this scope.  
• 
The following item(s) will be provided as deliverable(s):  
o 
404 Permit Application – Electronic Copy 
• 
The following assumptions have been made:  
o 
Aquatic Resources Inventory would be completed by two (2) staff during the growing 
season (typically May – September), dependent on weather.

RE: Cheyenne 19th Street intersection survey
Nathan, 
 
So here is a ballpark estimate for the project: 
 
1. Drone Photogrammetry & LiDAR Survey - $8,000 
• 
a. Set ±6 aerial targets for drone survey 
• 
b. Drone scan with a mapping camera 
• 
c. ECW Image File loaded into CAD 
• 
d. TIN Surface for areas outside road and adjacent parking lots 
 
2. Surface Extraction with Conventional- $2,380 
• 
Adding conventional survey data to drone surface 
 
3. Planimetric Drafting- $3,170 
• 
Planimetric Drafting - Surface linework at Client’s standard layers 
 
4. Conventional Survey Support – $7,000 
• 
GPS & Robotic Total Station to support drone mapping of hard surfaces including curb & gutter, street 
surfaces, sidewalks, pipe inverts, etc. 
• 
Boundary determination for street right-of-ways. 
• 
Establish Project Control 
 
Estimated cost for survey services is $20,550 
 
Look it over and let me know if I missed anything. 
 
Thanks, 
 
Cotton D. Jones, P.L.S., CFedS

Proposal for Geotechnical Engineering Services 
19th Street Drainage and Intersection Improvements | Cheyenne, Wyoming 
August 6, 2024 | Terracon Proposal No. P24245054 
 
 
 
 
Facilities | Environmental | Geotechnical | Materials 
Exhibit B - Scope of Services 
Our proposed Scope of Services consists of field exploration, laboratory testing, and 
project delivery. Our Scope of Services does not include providing geotechnical 
engineering recommendations or design criteria for the project. These services are 
described in the following sections. 
Field Exploration 
Based on input provided by Ayres, and our experience with similar projects in the 
vicinity of the project site, we propose the following field exploration program as 
described below:   
Number of 
Borings 
Planned Boring Depth (feet) 1 
Planned Location 2 
2 
50 
Within the existing pavement 
limits of Missile Drive 
1. Below existing ground surface. If sufficient subsurface information is not 
obtained to provide design-level recommendations after termination of 
planned boring depth, additional exploration and mobilization of 
specialty equipment may be required. Cost for specialty equipment 
mobilization and drilling, such as rock coring, will be provided upon 
your request. 
2. We understand Ayres will provide boring locations prior to our mobilization to 
the site.  
Pre-Exploration Site Visit: Prior to mobilizing our drilling equipment to the project 
site, a Terracon representative will visit the site to meet with a representative of the 
Ayres and/or the City of Cheyenne (as needed) to coordinate our field work, stake/mark 
boring locations, assess drill rig access/regress to/from the boring locations, and confirm 
public and private utilities have been appropriately marked or cleared. If conditions are 
identified during our pre-mobilization site visit that could negatively impact the field 
exploration, relative to private utilities and/or equipment access, we will contact you to 
discuss our concerns and options, and additional fees may apply. 
Boring Layout and Elevations: We will coordinate with your Project Surveyor to 
include locations and surface elevations in project information. 
Subsurface Exploration Procedures: We will advance the soil borings with a truck-
mounted drill rig using continuous-flight augers (solid-stem and/or hollow-stem, as 
necessary, depending on subsurface conditions). Typically, four samples are obtained in 
the upper 10 feet of each boring and at intervals of 5 feet thereafter. Sampling will be in 
STRUCTURE GEOTECHNICAL SCOPE
City of Cheyenne / Request for Proposal 16-24 
Page 8 of 14

Proposal for Geotechnical Engineering Services 
19th Street Drainage and Intersection Improvements | Cheyenne, Wyoming 
August 6, 2024 | Terracon Proposal No. P24245054 
 
 
 
 
Facilities | Environmental | Geotechnical | Materials 
general accordance with local standard procedures wherein modified California barrel or 
split-barrel samples are obtained. In the split barrel sampling procedure, a standard 2-
inch outer diameter split barrel sampling spoon is driven into the ground by a 140-pound 
automatic hammer falling a distance of 30 inches. The number of blows required to 
advance the sampling spoon the last 12 inches of a normal 18-inch penetration is 
recorded as the Standard Penetration Test (SPT) resistance value. The SPT resistance 
values, also referred to as N-values, are indicated on the boring logs at the test depths. 
A 2.5-inch O.D. split-barrel modified California sampling spoon with 2.0-inch I.D. tube 
lined sampler may also be used for sampling. The modified California barrel sampling 
procedures are similar to standard split spoon sampling procedure; however, blow 
counts are typically recorded for 6-inch intervals for a total of 12 inches of penetration. 
Bulk samples of auger cuttings may also be obtained from select boring locations and 
depths. The samples will be placed in appropriate containers, taken to our soil laboratory 
for testing, and classified by a Geotechnical Engineer.  
Groundwater levels will be observed and recorded during and after drilling operations. 
Provisions have not been made to obtain delayed groundwater readings. 
Our exploration team will prepare field boring logs as part of standard drilling operations 
including sampling depths, penetration distances, and other relevant sampling 
information. Field logs include visual classifications of materials observed during drilling 
and our interpretation of subsurface conditions between samples. Final boring logs, 
prepared from field logs, represent the Geotechnical Engineer's interpretation, and 
include modifications based on observations and laboratory tests. 
Property Disturbance: Terracon will make reasonable efforts to reduce damage to the 
property. However, it should be understood that in the normal course of our work some 
disturbance could occur including rutting of the ground surface and damage to 
landscaping and/or pavements.  
We will backfill borings with auger cuttings mixed with bentonite chips and/or pea gravel 
upon completion. Pavements will be patched with asphalt and/or concrete, as 
appropriate. Our services do not include repair of the site beyond backfilling our 
boreholes and patching existing pavements/surfaces. Excess auger cuttings will be 
removed from the roadway. Because backfill material often settles below the surface 
after a period, we recommend boreholes to be periodically checked and backfilled, if 
necessary. 
Safety 
Terracon is not aware of environmental concerns at this project site that would create 
health or safety hazards associated with our exploration program; thus, our Scope 
considers standard OSHA Level D Personal Protection Equipment (PPE) appropriate. Our 
City of Cheyenne / Request for Proposal 16-24 
Page 9 of 14

Proposal for Geotechnical Engineering Services 
19th Street Drainage and Intersection Improvements | Cheyenne, Wyoming 
August 6, 2024 | Terracon Proposal No. P24245054 
 
 
 
 
Facilities | Environmental | Geotechnical | Materials 
Scope of Services does not include environmental site assessment services, but 
identification of unusual or unnatural materials observed while drilling will be noted on 
our logs and discussed in our report. 
If suspected environmental impacts are observed or encountered during the field 
exploration, Ayres will be notified. The potential of environmental impact to field 
program represents a change in condition for the project. The environmental services 
related to continuing the field exploration are not included in this scope or services. 
Terracon is available to provide these services as a supplement to this proposal. 
Exploration efforts require borings into the subsurface; therefore, Terracon will comply 
with local regulations to request a utility location service through Wyoming One-Call. We 
will consult with Ayres/City of Cheyenne regarding potential utilities or other unmarked 
underground hazards. Based upon the results of this consultation, we will consider the 
need for alternative subsurface exploration methods as the safety of our field crew is a 
priority.  
Private utilities should be marked by the Ayres/City of Cheyenne and any information 
provided prior to commencement of field exploration. Terracon will not be responsible 
for damage to private utilities not disclosed to us, and we will not perform excavations 
without an understanding of the subsurface utilities present based upon markings made 
by the various responsible parties.  
Due to the site being developed, Terracon’s safety policy requires private utility 
services to be performed at all developed sites prior to performing subsurface 
exploration activities. We will subcontract private utility locating services to 
identify any private utilities in the vicinity of the proposed borings to reduce 
the risk of a utility strike. Fees for this service are included in our Scope of Services 
and can be found in Exhibit C – Compensation and Project Schedule of this 
proposal. 
Please note that the detection of underground utilities is dependent upon the 
composition and construction of the utility line; some utilities are comprised of non-
electrically conductive materials and may not be readily detected. The use of a private 
utility locate service would not relieve the landowner/client of their responsibilities in 
identifying private underground utilities.  
Site Access and Right-of-Way Permit: Terracon must be granted access to the public 
street right-of-way by the City of Cheyenne. We will work diligently with you to obtain 
all required Right-of-Way (ROW) permit and other necessary permits. Our proposed fees 
assume all fees associated with permitting through the City of Cheyenne will be the 
responsibility of Ayres and/or City of Cheyenne. Terracon will conduct field services 
during normal business hours (Monday through Friday between 7:00am and 5:00pm). If 
City of Cheyenne / Request for Proposal 16-24 
Page 10 of 14

Proposal for Geotechnical Engineering Services 
19th Street Drainage and Intersection Improvements | Cheyenne, Wyoming 
August 6, 2024 | Terracon Proposal No. P24245054 
 
 
 
 
Facilities | Environmental | Geotechnical | Materials 
our exploration must take place over a weekend or at night, please contact us so we can 
adjust our schedule and fee. 
Traffic Control: All our field operations, including performing private utility clearances 
at boring locations and drilling, will be conducted with the assistance of subcontracted 
professional traffic control services (S&J Signs). Fees for subcontracted traffic control 
services are provided in Exhibit C – Compensation and Project Schedule of this 
proposal. 
Laboratory Testing 
The project engineer will review field data and work with HDR to assign laboratory tests 
to understand the engineering properties of various soil strata. Exact types and number 
of tests cannot be defined until completion of fieldwork, but we anticipate the following 
laboratory testing may be performed:  
■ 
Water content 
■ 
Dry unit weight 
■ 
Atterberg limits 
■ 
Grain size analysis  
■ 
One-dimensional swell/collapse (if applicable) 
■ 
Moisture-density relationship 
■ 
California Bearing Ratio (CBR) 
■ 
Chemical analyses – pH, soluble sulfate, sulfides, soluble chloride, RedOx, 
electrical resistivity, and total salts 
Our laboratory testing program includes examination of soil samples by an 
engineer/geologist. Based on the results of our field and laboratory programs, we will 
describe and classify soil samples in accordance with the Unified Soil Classification 
System (USCS). 
Engineering and Project Delivery 
The results of our field and laboratory programs will be evaluated, and a geotechnical 
data report will be prepared under the supervision of a licensed professional engineer. 
The geotechnical data report will provide the following:  
■ 
Site Location and Exploration Plans  
■ 
Subsurface exploration procedures  
■ 
Description of subsurface conditions 
■ 
Boring logs with:

In addition to an emailed data report, your project will also be delivered using the 
Terracon Compass website. Upon initiation, we provide you and your design team with 
the necessary link and password to access the website (if not previously registered). 
Each project includes a calendar to track the schedule, an interactive site map, a listing 
of team members, access to the project documents as they are uploaded to the site, and 
a collaboration portal. We welcome the opportunity to have project kickoff conversations 
with the team to discuss key elements of the project and demonstrate features of the 
portal. The typical delivery process includes the following: 
■ 
Project Planning – Proposal information, schedule, and anticipated exploration 
plan  
■ 
Site Characterization – Findings of the site exploration and laboratory results 
■ 
Geotechnical Data Report  
When services are complete, we upload a printable version of our completed 
Geotechnical Data report, including the Professional Engineer’s seal and signature, which 
documents our services. Previous submittals, collaboration, and the report are 
maintained in our system. This allows future reference and integration into subsequent 
aspects of our services as the project goes through final design and construction. 
Additional Services 
In addition to the services noted above, the following are often associated with 
geotechnical engineering services. Fees for services noted above do not include the 
following:  
Environmental Assessments: Our Scope for this project does not include, either 
specifically or by implication, an environmental assessment of the site intended to 
identify or quantify potential site contaminants. If the client/owner is concerned about 
the potential for such conditions, an environmental site assessment should be 
conducted. We can provide a proposal for an environmental assessment, if desired.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
August 21, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and The Design 
Studio, Inc. (TDSi), for the Restoration and Remodel of the Cheyenne Pumphouse 
 ______________________________________________ 
Three (3) Proposers responded to Request for Proposal (RFP) #15-24, for the Restoration and 
Remodel of the Cheyenne Pumphouse, Project. 
  
The proposals were received from:  
1. The Design Studio, Inc. (TDSi) 
2. Anderson Hallas Architects 
3. Plan One Architects  
 
Upon review of the proposals by the evaluation panel, the group has recommended acceptance of 
the proposal submitted by The Design Studio, Inc. (TDSi), for a project award amount of 
$424,200.00 
 
Basis for award was based on a Qualifications and Cost based review and scoring of proposals. 
 
Funding:   
Proposed Funding Source – General Fund Reserves 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 15-24 
Restoration and Remodel of the Cheyenne Pumphouse 
Page 1 of 7 
City Contract No. ____________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
RESTORATION AND REMODEL OF THE CHEYENNE PUMPHOUSE 
 
This Professional Services Agreement (“Agreement”) is made and entered into by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and The Design Studio, inc. (TDSi), located at 1610 Pioneer Avenue, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming 82001 (“Consultant”).  This Agreement shall become effective upon the date of the 
last signature affixed hereto. 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by 
and between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the Restoration and Remodel 
of the Cheyenne Pumphouse, in accordance with City of Cheyenne Request for 
Proposal (RFP) #15-24, incorporated herein by reference, and the Consultant’s 
submitted proposal, incorporated herein by reference. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed as follows: Completion of 
Construction Documents (CD’s) April 15, 2025, with Schedule for Bidding/Negotiation 
(B/N) and Construction Administration (CA) Phases to Be Determined.  The Consultant 
shall perform its services as expeditiously as is consistent with such professional skill and 
care and the orderly progress of the Project. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the 
Consultant will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant Four Hundred 
Twenty-Four Thousand, Two Hundred Dollars ($424,200.00), as stated in Exhibit A 
– Price Proposal.   
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the 
Consultant, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services 
not meeting such a standard. 
 
 
 
10:53 am, Aug 21 2024

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 15-24 
Restoration and Remodel of the Cheyenne Pumphouse 
Page 2 of 7 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement 
in accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
6. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of 
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to 
alcohol and drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers, and other 
consultants under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and 
sub-consultants, are required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and 
Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites 
occupied by the Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the 
Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the 
requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the 
requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees, or 
sub-consultants are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated. 
 
7. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
To the fullest extent permitted by law, Consultant shall indemnify the City of Cheyenne, 
its officers, directors, partners, employees, and representatives, from and against losses, 
damages, and judgments arising from claims by third parties, including reasonable 
attorneys’ fees and expenses recoverable under applicable law, but only to the extent they 
are found to be caused by a negligent act, error, or omission of Consultant or 
Consultant’s officers, directors, members, partners, agents, employees, or subconsultants 
in the performance of services under this Agreement. 
 
8. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness, or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000 
per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Agreement, 
as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or 
employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from 
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 15-24 
Restoration and Remodel of the Cheyenne Pumphouse 
Page 3 of 7 
under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be 
provided pursuant to an “occurrence” policy. 
 
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
 
 
a. 
Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, 
provide the City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such 
coverage is or shall be in effect at the time the Consultant begins the 
provision of services under this Agreement; 
 
 
b. 
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, 
the Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City 
with a new certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or 
shall be in effect during all periods of time that Consultant will provide 
services under this Agreement; 
 
 
c. 
Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five 
(5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement; and 
 
 
d. 
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” 
coverage prior to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs 
a., b., or c. of this paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City 
advance written notification of the termination of said coverage and shall 
provide the City with an endorsement for an extended reporting period 
(“tail coverage”) which shall be in effect for a period of time not less than 
five (5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and 
professional liability insurance policies. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the 
above-stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the 
Agreement.  It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify 
in writing the city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or 
occurrence that has resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change 
to any insurance coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be 
construed to be a breach of this Agreement. 
 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of insurance 
policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 15-24 
Restoration and Remodel of the Cheyenne Pumphouse 
Page 4 of 7 
The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall 
not affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 
 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum 
A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
9. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin, or sex. 
 
10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, the Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a 
written statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, 
and work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing 
of any payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities 
that may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing 
in this Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or 
employees to act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any 
obligation of any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, 
debts, and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this 
Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default 
remains uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 15-24 
Restoration and Remodel of the Cheyenne Pumphouse 
Page 5 of 7 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
The term of this Agreement shall be from date of execution to April 15, 2025.  The 
parties may by mutual written agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 
 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. 
Upon receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, 
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as 
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the 
Consultant for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of 
termination. Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, 
the City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of 
default of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default 
to commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear 
its own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
18. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its 
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary 
for the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the 
Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et 
seq., and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, 
enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of 
governmental immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not 
be strictly construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to 
governmental or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental 
immunity. 
 
19. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are 
performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be limited 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 15-24

to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring 
experts and consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in 
progress; to examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this 
Agreement; and to observe personnel in every phase of performance of the related work. 
 
20. 
OWNERSHIP OF DOCUMENTS AND WORK PRODUCT 
All deliverables by the Consultant, all work product of the Consultant, all notes, 
calculations, memoranda, or any other documentation generated by the Consultant 
pursuant to the scope of this Agreement, shall be the property of the City of Cheyenne 
and shall be provided to the City of Cheyenne immediately upon request. 
 
21. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With 
Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age 
Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto, 
and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color, 
race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy or qualifying disability in connection 
with the performance under this Agreement. 
 
22. 
FORCE MAJEURE. 
The performance of the Agreement by either party shall be subject to force majeure 
including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural disaster, war or threat of 
war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized strikes, governmental 
regulation or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by the World Health 
Organization, the Centers for Disease Control, or local government authority or health 
agencies (including, but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-19, H1N1, or similar 
infectious diseases), curtailment of transportation facilities, or other similar occurrence 
beyond the control of the parties, where any of those factors, circumstances, situations, or 
conditions or similar ones prevent, dissuade, or unreasonably delay the performance 
required by this Agreement. The Agreement may be cancelled by either party, without 
liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any unused deposits or amounts paid shall be 
refunded, for any one or more of the above reasons, by written notice to the other party. 
 
23. 
PROFESSIONAL REGISTRATION 
The Consultant shall endorse, as required by law, plans and reports prepared under this 
Agreement, and shall affix thereto his or her seal of professional registration, showing 
that he or she is licensed to practice in the State of Wyoming, if necessary. 
 
24. 
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement consisting of seven (7) pages, Exhibit A – Price Proposal, consisting of 
nine (9) pages, Request for Proposal RFP 15-24, issued via OpenGov E-Procurement

out of town travel and subsistence expenses; 
 
reproduction costs for documents for other than the Architect’s use or record; 
 
postage and shipping charges. 
 
o Hourly Rates and Reimbursable Expenses Rates are subject to review and change on a periodic 
basis, or as circumstances require.

prepared. The Final Drainage Report will be submitted with the Civil Engineering

MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins, City of Cheyenne 
Members of the City Council, City of Cheyenne 
 
FROM: 
Erin Gates, PE, Staff Engineer  
 
DATE: 
August 21, 2024 
 
SUBJECT: 
Drainage easement granted by Kenrich, LLC to the City of Cheyenne along the 
Christensen Road corridor. 
 
 
Please find attached documentation for a permanent drainage easement grated to the City of 
Cheyenne from Kenrich, LLC. This drainage easement is necessary due to the encroachment of 
drainage infrastructure associated with Christesen Road onto private property. An appraisal has 
been completed for the 446 square feet of affected area. The diminution value of the parcel is 
$424.00. Compensation for this easement will come from the 2017 Specific Purpose Option Tax 
Fund, GL 030-20-2033-42-21301. Your approval of this drainage easement is respectfully 
requested. 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
CITY ENGINEER’S OFFICE 
2101 O’Neil Avenue  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 

Page 1 of 3 
DRAINAGE E ASEMENT 
 
KNOW ALL MEN BY THESE PRESENTS that the undersigned, Kenrich, LLC, a 
Wyoming Limited Liability Company, hereinafter referred to as "Grantor," in consideration of the 
sum of Ten Dollars and other good and valuable consideration as provided in that certain Drainage 
Easement Agreement of even date herewith, the receipt of which is hereby acknowledged, and the 
further consideration of performance of the covenants and agreements by the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming ("City"), a Municipal Corporation, its successors and assigns, hereinafter referred to as 
"Grantee," as hereinafter set out and expressed, hereby grants, bargains, sells and conveys to the 
Grantee, a permanent drainage easement ("Easement") to construct, reconstruct, operate, maintain, 
repair, remove and replace a drainageway and appurtenant features thereto, including ditches, 
swales, catchments, riprap, catch basins, storm drains, storm sewers, culverts, detention facilities, 
and retention facilities ("Facilities") on, over, through and across certain lands, situate in the 
NE1/4NE1/4, Section 25, T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, said easement 
being more particularly described as follows: 
 
An easement for a drainageway and appurtenant facilities located in the Northeast Quarter of 
Section 25, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, 
more particularly described as follows: 
 
Beginning at a point on the East line of the Northeast Quarter of Section 25, from which the 
Northeast Corner of said Section 25 bears N0°04’21”E, a distance of 727.22 feet; thence 
N89°55’39”W, a distance of 50.00 feet to the True Point of Beginning, being on the West Right 
of Way line of Christensen Road; thence S89°58’50”E, a distance of 17.86 feet; thence 
S0°01’10”E, a distance of 25.00 feet; thence N89°58’50”E, a distance of 17.82 feet to a point on 
the West Right of Way line of Christensen Road; N0°04’21”E, a distance of 25.00 feet to the 
point of beginning.  
 
Said Easement contains 0.01 acres more or less. See Exhibit A attached hereto. 
TO HAVE AND HOLD unto said Grantee so long as the "Facilities" shall be maintained, 
together with the right of ingress to and egress from said premises for the purpose of constructing, 
reconstructing, operating, maintaining, repairing, removing, and replacing said Facilities, or the 
removal thereof in whole or in part, at the will of the Grantee, it being the intention of the parties 
hereto that the Grantor is hereby granting the uses herein specified without divesting the Grantor of 
the right to enjoy the above-described premises, subject only to the rights of the Grantee to use the 
same for the purposes herein expressed. 
 
As further consideration of this agreement, the Grantor herein agrees as follows: 
 
1. 
The Grantor shall not construct or place any structure or building, on the Easement. 
Grantor may otherwise use the Permanent Easement area as long as such use does not interfere with 
Grantee's use. 
 
2. 
That Grantee will replace or rebuild any and all unusual or excessive damage to the 
easement property which shall be occasioned by the operation and maintenance of said Facilities 
under and through the above-described premises.

shall inure to the benefit of, and be binding upon the legal representatives, heirs, devisees, successors

granted

(g)  "IS" means the agency's account within the internal service fund;

9-3-507;

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-08-06_TotalExtraction_41841627.pdf
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins, City of Cheyenne 
Members of the City Council, City of Cheyenne 
 
FROM: 
Erin Gates, PE, Staff Engineer  
 
DATE: 
August 7, 2024 
 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Service Agreement Contract Modification No. 1 between the City of 
Cheyenne and TPT Trails for additional design-build services and trail 
maintenance services for the Belvoir Ranch Phase 2, Design-Build Trail project.  
 
 
Please find attached the proposed contract modification for the above-referenced project. 
Engineering and Construction staff have discussed the additional work of designing and 
constructing approximately 35,200 linear feet of single-track trail. The proposed additional 
services will be provided at the original contract pricing and will require an extension of the 
completion date to June 15, 2024. Additionally, this contract modification will be further beneficial 
to the City, as it will provide trial maintenance for the single-track trails completed under this 
contract and the anticipated universal access trail through November 30, 2026. The additional costs 
associated with this work will be $216,000.00. Funding for this project will come from the Belvoir 
Recreation Fund, GL 015-21-2111-45-21301. Your approval of this contract modification is 
respectfully requested. 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
CITY ENGINEER’S OFFICE 
2101 O’Neil Avenue  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 

CITY OF CHEYENNE 
CONTRACT MODIFICATION 
 
Contract Modification: 
#1 
Project: 
 
 
Belvoir Ranch Phase 2, Design-Build Trail 
Project Number: 
 
Contract # 7975 
Contractor: 
 
 
TPT Trails 
Date Prepared: 
 
July 30, 2024 
 
The above referenced contract is amended or modified as set forth in the following and these 
amendments and modifications of the plans and specifications are agreed to by the parties. All other 
aspects of the original agreement remain unchanged and in full force and effect. 
 
I. Description of Changes 
 
Changes to the contract will increase the quantity of single-track trail designed and constructed 
to include approximately 35,200 additional linear feet at $5.00 per linear foot as shown in 
Exhibit A. This will complete the construction of the single-track trail network in the southern 
portion of the Belvoir Ranch Trail System for mountain biking and hiking. 
 
II. New Contract Items 
 
TPT Trails will provide two years of maintenance of the single-track and universal access trail 
(to be included once constructed). Maintenance will include repairing drainage and erosion 
issues, vandalism, damage to signs and other constructed features. Maintenance will also include 
grubbing and pruning all vegetation in the trail corridor, rototilling the trail tread as necessary, 
raking, and clean-up. Trail maintenance will be paid as lump sum and on a quarterly basis, for a 
total of $40,000.00 for the two-year maintenance period. 
 
III. Schedule 
 
Construction of the additional single-track trail shall be completed by June 30, 2024. 
Maintenance will commence December 1, 2024 and continue through November 30, 2026. 
 
IV. Summary 
 
Original Contract Amount ................................................................................$ 219,672.93 
Previous Modifications ................................................................................................$ 0.00 
Contract Amount Prior to this Modification .....................................................$ 219,672.93 
This Modification .............................................................................................$ 216,000.00 
New Contract Amount ......................................................................................$ 435,672.93 
 
V. GL Account Information 
 
If requesting a modification to the contract price, please list GL account # to cover the balance of 
this modification (if using more than (1) GL account, list the dollar amount for each respective 
account): 
 
GL# 015-21-2111-45-21301  Amount: $ 216,000.00 
 
 
 
 
     
9:30 am, Aug 07 2024

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
August 21, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #S-2-25 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
Reiman Corp. for the Depot Renovation project 
______________________________________________ 
Bid #S-2-25 was solicited for the Depot Renovation Project. 
 
Seven (7) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  The Bids were received 
from: 
1. Borderline Fencing Inc. 
a. Bid Rejected 
2. Garcia Contracting, LLC 
a. Bid Rejected 
3. Reiman Corp. 
4. GH Phipps Wyoming LLC 
5. Richardson Construction, Inc 
6. S&S Builders LLC 
7. Construction Specialists, Inc 
 
The bids were reviewed by Jason Sanchez (CRE), Sam Payne (CRE), and TDSi (Project Architect) 
and their recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from Reiman Corp. 
 
The total Bid Award is $1,426,265.00. 
 
Project Completion is scheduled for March 31, 2025. 
 
Funding:  
032-21-2113-45-43110 
2021 Special Purpose Option Tax (SPOT 2021 / Downtown Projects) 

1610 Pioneer Avenue 
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
T) 307.775.9970    www.tdsi.us
2BU 
Scope of Work Included in 
Cheyenne Depot Renovation 
Cheyenne, Wuoming 
Scope of Project: 
1. Renovation of the existing First Floor Public Restrooms located between Depot Lobby
and Accomplice Brewing. The scope of work includes replacement of restroom fixtures
(toilets, urinals, lavatories, countertops, and accessories), update of finishes, and
adjustments to provide proper handicap access and to address vandalism.
2. Repair and/or replacement of the exterior Depot Lobby doors (5 locations total). This
work will repair existing doors, and in two locations will replace the doors and sidelight
configuration to allow wider access to allow movement of items into and out of the
Depot Lobby.
3. Development of a Pedestrian Patio on the southwest side of the building. This work will
incorporate brick pavers to match those found elsewhere in the Depot Plaza, shade
structures, tables, benches and other amenities. The work will also revise landings at
doors to ensure proper handicap access. This patio will be accessible from the Depot
Lobby and will provide additional and highly desirable visitor space for train watching,
etc. and will be available for Depot or private events.
4. Installation of decorative metal fence along the UP Railroad right of way from the
parking area west of the Depot to the east end of the Depot property. This will further
enhance the Depot appearance and visitor experience. The development of the
Pedestrian Patio and the upgrade of the fence will be significant improvements to south
side of the Depot property.
5. Renovation of the kitchen area adjacent to the Depot Lobby. The renovation work will
update this area to allow use for catering and/or a concessionaire.
6. Additional power receptacles are being added at the exterior of building.
7. Repair of the in-floor Train Map display located in Depot Lobby. This unique feature has
been inoperable for some time and will be renovated to bring it back to life.
8. Replacement of exterior door lock with keypads/access control in selected location to
enhance security at the facility.

ARTICLE 19 MISCELLANEOUS PROVISIONS 
§ 19.1 Assignment of Contract
Neither party to the Contract shall assign the Contract without written consent of the other, except that the Owner
may, without consent of the Contractor, assign the Contract to a lender providing construction financing for the
Project if the lender assumes the Owner's rights and obligations under the Contract Documents. The Contractor shall
execute all consents reasonably required to facilitate such assignment.
§ 19.2 Governing Law
The Contract shall be governed by the law of the place where the Project is located, excluding that jurisdiction's
choice oflaw rules. If the parties have selected arbitration as the method of binding dispute resolution, the Federal
Arbitration Act shall govern Section 21.6.
§ 19.3 Tests and Inspections
Tests, inspections, and approvals of portions of the Work required by the Contract Documents or by applicable laws,
statutes, ordinances, codes, rules and regulations, or lawful orders of public authorities shall be made at an
appropriate time. Unless otherwise provided, the Contractor shall make arrangements for such tests, inspections, and
approvals with an independent testing laboratory or entity acceptable to the Owner, or with the appropriate public
authority, and shall bear all related costs of tests, inspections, and approvals. The Contractor shall give the Architect
timely notice of when and where tests and inspections are to be made so that the Architect may be present for such
procedures. The Owner shall bear costs of tests, inspections, or approvals that do not become requirements until
after bids are received or negotiations concluded. The Owner shall directly arrange and pay for tests, inspections, or
approvals where building codes or applicable laws or regulations so require.

regardless of whether the same were furnished or performed by the Contractor or by
any of its Subcontractors of any tier. Upon receipt of written notice from the Architect
or the Owner of a breach of warranty during the applicable warranty period, the
affected item shall be redesigned, repaired, or replaced, by the Contractor, and it
shall perform such tests as the Architect or the Owner may require to verify that such
redesigned, repaired or replacement comply with the requirements of the Contract.
As to redesigned, repaired, or replaced work against defective design, materials and
workmanship shall be guaranteed for a period of one (1) year following date of
acceptance of such work.

a.
14.5.1 Claims for increase in the Contract Time shall set forth in detail the circumstances
that form the basis for the Claim, the date upon which each cause of delay began to affect
the progress of the Work, the date upon which each cause of delay ceased to affect the
progress of the Work and the number of days’ increase in the Contract Time claimed as a
consequence of each such cause of delay. The Contractor shall provide such supporting
documentation as the Owner may require including, where appropriate, a revised
construction schedule indicating all the activities affected by the circumstances forming the
basis of the Claim.
b.
14.5.2 The Contractor shall not be entitled to a separate increase in the Contract Time for
each one of the number of causes of delay which may have concurrent or interrelated
effects on the progress of the Work, or for concurrent delays due to the fault of the
Contractor.
c.
14.5.3 Claims by the Contractor for increase in the Contract Time, if not part of a Proposal
Request and subsequent Change Order, shall be initiated by written notice to the Architect
as soon as possible, but in no event later than 21 days after the occurrence of the event
giving rise to the Claim, or 21 days after the claimant first recognizes the condition giving
rise to the Claim, which ever is later.
d.
14.5.4 Adverse Weather Delays. The Contractor is expected to anticipate a certain
amount of adverse weather, based upon the historic weather patterns of the projects
location, when preparing its bid, and subsequently its schedule. Only weather conditions
that would be unanticipated or unusually severe, and that detrimentally impact current
outdoor tasks on the project's critical path schedule, will be considered for extension of the
Contract Time. The method used to determine additional time for adverse weather
conditions shall be based upon methodology utilized by the Wyoming Department of
Transportation, as described below.
1)
14.5.4.1 Additional time for adverse weather during the original contract time period
may be allowed if the actual number of adverse days encountered in a month
exceeds the normal number of adverse weather days for that month shown in the
table below.
2)
14.5.4.2 Adverse Weather Days are defined as week day on which, in the Architect's
opinion, unanticipated or unusually severe weather conditions detrimentally impact
current outdoor tasks on the project's critical path schedule by not allowing
construction operations by the current work force to proceed for a minimum of five
hours during the hours between 7:00 am and 6:00 pm.
3)
14.5.4.3 Architect will determine the number of actual adverse weather days in
excess of the number shown in the table below for each month, as well as the total to
date. The Contractor shall submit all exceptions to the the Architect's determination
within ten days of the date of the Architects determination.
4)
14.5.4.4 The following table indicates the number of normal adverse weather days by
month, or portion thereof. Saturdays, Sundays and holidays will not be considered for
adverse weather days.

placed with it pursuant to this act on any contract of more than fifty thousand dollars

c.

$ 1,000,000.00 Each Accident
3.
17.1.6 The limit for Employers' Liability insurance shall be as follows:
a.
$ 500,000.00 Each Accident
b.
$ 500,000.00 Disease - Each Emoployee
c.
$ 500,000.00 Disease - Policy Limit
4.
Delete Section 17.1.7
5.
Delete Section 17.1.8
6.
Delete Section 17.1.9
7.
Add the following to Section 17.1.10.1 to Section 17.1.10:
a.
If this insurance is written on a Commercial General Liability policy form, the certificates
shall be ACORD form 25, completed and supplemented in accordance with AIA Document
G715™–2017, Instruction Sheet and Supplemental Attachment for ACORD Certificate of
Insurance 25.
8.
Add the following to Section 17.1.10.2 to Section 17.1.10:
a.
15.4.3 The Owner and Architect shall be named as additional insureds.
9.
Add the following to Sections 17.1.14.1, 17.1.14.2 and 17.1.14.3 to Section 17.1.14 Other
Insurance Provided by the Contractor:
a.
17.1.14.1 The Contractor shall at the Contractor's own expense provide insurance
coverage for materials stored off the site after written approval of the Owner at the value
established in the approval, and also for portions of the Work in transit until such materials
are permanently attached to the Work.
b.
17.1.14.2 The insurance required by Section 17.1 is not intended to cover machinery,
tools or equipment owned or rented by the Contractor that are utilized in the performance
of the Work but not incorporated into the permanent improvements. The Contractor shall,
at the Contractor's own expense, provide insurance coverage for owned or rented
machinery, tools or equipment.
c.
17.1.14.3 Modify the first sentence of Section 5.2 as follows: "The contractor shall provide
property insurance." Add the following sentences:
1)
If the Owner is damaged by the failure of the Contractor to purchase and maintain
such insurance without so notifying the Owner in writing, then the Contractor shall
bear all reasonable costs attributable thereto.
Exhibit B

Section 00 7300 - Supplementary Conditions 
 24.2.451 - Cheyenne Depot Renovations 
Page 8 of 10 
Copyright TDSi 2024.07.10
10.
Add the following to Sections 17.1.14.4 to Section 17.1.14 Other Insurance Provided by the
Contractor:
a.
17.1.14.4 Property Insurance
1)
17.1.14.4.1 The Contractor shall purchase and maintain, from an insurance company
or insurance companies lawfully authorized to issue insurance in the jurisdiction
where the Project is located, property insurance written on a builder's risk “all-risks”
completed value or equivalent policy form and sufficient to cover the total value of the
entire Project on a replacement cost basis. The Contractor’s property insurance
coverage shall be no less than the amount of the initial Contract Sum, plus the value
of subsequent Modifications and labor performed or materials or equipment supplied
by others. The property insurance shall be maintained until Substantial Completion
and thereafter as provided in Section 17.1.14.4.2, unless otherwise provided in the
Contract Documents or otherwise agreed in writing by the parties to this Agreement.
This insurance shall include the interests of the Owner, Contractor, Subcontractors,
and Sub-subcontractors in the Project as insureds. This insurance shall include the
interests of mortgagees as loss payees.
2)
 17.1.14.4.2 Unless the parties agree otherwise, upon Substantial Completion, and
proof by the Owner of permanent property insurance, the Contractor may terminate
the insurance required by Section 17.1.14.4.1.
3)
17.1.14.4.3 If the insurance required by this Section 17.14.4 is subject to deductibles
or self-insured retentions, the Contractor shall be responsible for all loss not covered
because of such deductibles or retentions.
11.
Add the following to Section 17.3 Performance Bond and Payment Bond:
a.
17.3.3 The Contractor shall furnish bonds covering faithful performance of the Contract
and payment of obligations arising thereunder. Bonds may be obtained through the
Contractor's usual source and the cost thereof shall be included in the Contract Sum. The
amount of each bond shall be equal to One Hundred percent (100%) of the Contract Sum.
1)
17.3.3.2 The Contractor shall deliver the required bonds to the Owner not later than
the date given in Section 00 3100 - Available Project Information for 'Contractor's
Post Bid Submittals Due On Or Before'.
2)
17.3.3.3 The Contractor shall require the attorney-in-fact who executes the required
bonds on behalf of the surety to affix thereto a certified and current copy of the power
of attorney.
I.
ARTICLE 18: Correction of Work
1.
Add the following Section 18.2.1 to Article 18.2.
a.
18.2.1 Upon request by the Owner and prior to the expiration of one year from the date of
Substantial Completion, the Architect will conduct and the Contractor shall attend a
meeting with the Owner to review the facility operations and performance.
J.
ARTICLE 19: Miscellaneous Provisions
1.
Add the following sections:
a.
19.7 Governmental Immunity
1)
The Owner and its officials and employees do not waive governmental immunity by
entering into this Agreement and specifically retain all Immunities and defenses
available to them as Governmental Entities pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et
seq, and all other applicable laws.  Further, the Owner fully retains all Immunities and
defenses provided by law with regard to any action, whether in tort, contract. or any
other theory of law, based on this Agreement.  The Owner does waive its
governmental Immunities solely for the enforcement of the terms and conditions of
this Agreement.
Exhibit B

Section 00 7300 - Supplementary Conditions 
24.2.451 - Cheyenne Depot Renovations 
 
Copyright TDSi 2024.07.10
Page 9 of 10 
b.
19.8 Force Majeure
1)
The performance of the Agreement by either party shall be subject to force majeure
including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural disaster, war or threat of
war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized strikes, governmental
regulation or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by the World Health
Organization, the Centers for Disease Control, or local government authority or
health agencies (including, but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-19, H1N1,
or similar infectious diseases), curtailment of transportation facilities, or other similar
occurrence beyond the control of the parties, where any of those factors,
circumstances, situations, or conditions or similar ones prevent, dissuade, or
unreasonably delay the performance required by this Agreement. The Agreement
may be cancelled by either party, without liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any
unused deposits or amounts paid shall be refunded, for any one or more of the above
reasons, by written notice to the other party.
c.
19.9 Value Engineering
1)
A Value Engineering Proposal (“VEP”) is a creative proposal initiated by the
Contractor to amend the Contract to use an alternate method, design, material, or
similar element, to reduce the project’s cost or improve its outcome for both the
Owner and the Contractor’s benefit.
2)
The Contractor may submit a VEP for consideration by the Owner after the Owner
awards the contract. The Contractor shall submit a VEP in accordance with the
procedure outlined in the current edition of the Wyoming Department of
Transportation Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Construction (“WYDOT
Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge Construction”), Subsection 104.3.4,
“Submitting a VECP”.
3)
The Owner will not consider VEPs that are cost reductions resulting from corrections
to design errors; that are inconsistent with the Owner’s design policies and criteria for
the project; or that may require excessive time or cost for review.
4)
If the Owner and the Architect accept a VEP which results in a net reduction in the
contract price, the Contractor will share proportionally with the Owner in the net
savings (Owner 50%; Contractor 50%), less the cost of the Architect’s time required
to evaluate the VEP. Net savings are defined as savings available after deducting
VEP evaluation costs.
PART 2 PRODUCTS - NOT USED
PART 3 EXECUTION - NOT USED
END OF SECTION 00 7300

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-07-02_TotalExtraction_16831961.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
July 8, 2024  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Tom Segrave and Jeff White. Absent: Scott 
Roybal Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda). Mr. Segrave moved to approve remaining items, seconded by 
Mr. Esquibel.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the 
exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on June 24, 2024.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MUB Mixed-Use Business to CB Community 
Business for land located northeast of the intersection of Cheyenne Street and Ridge Road.  
Reporting for Public Services Committee Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present. (#4580) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Section 10.16.150, Parking Violation 
Hearings and Appeal Procedures, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
modifying the procedures governing the Administrative Review of Parking Citations, making 
conforming amendments and repealing an obsolete provision. Dr. Rinne moved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. 
 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Creating Section 10.60.015, Paid On-Street Parking 
Districts; Establishment, Administration, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, establishing the Paid On-Street Parking Districts Program and making conforming 
amendments as specified. Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave. Dr. Aldrich moved to amend item A3 under 10.04.020 changing the central business 
district boundaries by extending it north to 24th Street and limiting it to Warren on the east, 
seconded by Dr. Rinne. Amendment failed. Voting “no” – all members of the governing body 
present with the exception of Dr. Aldrich voting “yes”. Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present.  Public comment was made by: Matt Hanger. Comments 

were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Johnson, Mr. 
Laybourn, and Mr. Segrave.  
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the submission of an application by the City of Cheyenne 
for federal funding through the Transportation Alternatives Program administered by the 
Wyoming Department of Transportation for the purpose of construction for a Greenway path for 
pedestrians and bicycles from the Sun Valley Open Space into Kiwanis Community Park. Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6416)   
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Supporting the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board in the application for 
and acceptance of federal assistance from the Federal Aviation Administration under the 
Bipartisan Infrastructure Law to assist in the rehabilitation of the air traffic control tower. Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6417) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Certifying compliance with Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402 for the City-
initiated annexation of tracts of land situate in portions of Sections 4 and 5, T13N, R67W, 6th 
P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (also known as ‘North Range Business Park’, located northwest 
of the I-80 and Roundtop Road interchange) – referred to Public Services Committee. (SPONSOR 
– MR. ROYBAL) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Expressing the support of the Governing Body for the submission of a 
petition by the Governing Body to the Downtown Development Authority for inclusion of property 
in the Downtown Development District – referred to Public Services Committee.  (SPONSORS – 
MR. LAYBOURN, MR. ROYBAL, MR. SEGRAVE AND MR. WHITE) 
 
[CA]
