ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN 
ORDINANCE 
AMENDING 
SECTION 
10.16.150, 
PARKING 
VIOLATION HEARINGS AND APPEAL PROCEDURES, OF THE 
MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, 
MODIFYING 
THE 
PROCEDURES 
GOVERNING 
THE 
ADMINISTRATIVE REVIEW OF PARKING CITATIONS, MAKING 
CONFORMING AMENDMENTS AND REPEALING AN OBSOLETE 
PROVISION." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 10.16.150, Parking Violation Administrative Review Procedures, 
of Chapter 10.16, Arrest and Citation Procedure, and Section 10.60.040, Fine Schedule, of Chapter 
10.60, Parking Limitations, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
are hereby amended to read: 
 
10.16.150 - Parking violation hearings and appeal administrative review procedures. 
 
A. Civil actions to enforce this chapter may be adjudicated by an administrative hearing 
officer actively licensed to practice law in the State of Wyoming designated by the mayor. 
 
A. Any person liable for a parking citation may request administrative review of the citation 
by contacting the police department parking administration manager in person, by 
telephone or mail not more than fifteen (15) days after the date of the issuance of the 
citation. The request shall specify the citation for which the person seeks review and 
provide the reasons for seeking administrative review. Under this section, a request for 
review made by telephone shall be considered received as of the day it was made if received 
by a police department employee between the hours of 8:00 a.m. and 5:00 p.m. of any day, 
except on Saturday, Sunday or a city holiday. 
 
B. A hearing for a parking violation may be heard by an administrative hearing officer. The 
administrative hearing officer may Upon receipt of a request for administrative review 
under subsection (A) of this section, the parking administration manager shall promptly 
transmit the request to the director of the compliance department or his or her designee. 
The director shall review the citation and the facts and circumstances surrounding the 
issuance of the citation and make such orders written determinations as may be necessary 
and proper to dispose of such cases to appropriately and impartially decide the controversy. 
, including reduction or increase of fine amounts notwithstanding the schedule of fines set 
forth by the governing body and this section. 
 
C. Any defendant who has been issued a parking citation Any person aggrieved by a the 
decision in a parking violation hearing of the director of the compliance department or his 
or her designee as provided in this subsection may seek judicial review in the manner 
8:31 am, Jun 18, 2024

 
Page 2 of 3 
 
provided by the Wyoming Administrative Procedure Act and the Wyoming Rules of 
Appellate Procedure. 
 
DC. 
If a defendant, who has been issued a parking citation, fails to request a hearing 
within fifteen (15) days after the issuance of the citation, the allegations in the citation Any 
person that fails to request a hearing within fifteen (15) days after the issuance of the 
citation as required under subsection (A) of this section shall be deemed to have waived 
their right to administrative review and to have admitted and to the allegations contained 
in the parking citation. After the expiration of the fifteen (15) day appeal period, the police 
department parking administration manager shall enter judgment for the city and impose a 
civil sanction in the amount of the issued citation and any fine increases in accordance with 
as provided by Section 10.16.090(A) of this chapter. 
 
E. 
If a defendant , who has requested a hearing in accordance with Section 10.16.140, fails to 
appear at their scheduled hearing, the police department parking administration manager shall 
enter judgment for the city and impose a civil sanction in the amount of the issued citation and any 
fine increases in accordance with Section 10.16.090 A. 
 
10.60.040 - Fine schedule. 
 
Any such fine to be imposed pursuant to a notice of violation issued under Section 10.60.030 
of this chapter, including any applicable administrative fees, shall be determined specified by 
resolution of the governing body, including any administrative fees, and shall be made available 
to the general public in the form of a fine schedule. Administrative fees shall be factored into the 
total amount of the fine before any late payment penalties are applied in accordance with Section 
10.16.090(A) of the city code. 
 
A. Administrative fees shall be factored into the total amount of the fine before any late 
payment penalties are applied in accordance with Section 10.16.090A. 
 
B. Court costs of ten dollars ($10.00) may be assessed against those requesting a hearing 
in accordance with Section 10.16.140 . 
 
Section 2.  That Section 10.16.140, Request for Hearing in Parking Cases, of Chapter 
10.16, Arrest and Citation Procedure, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic is repealed.  
 
10.16.140 - Request for hearing in parking cases. 
 
The person or persons liable for the parking violation may request a hearing for consideration 
of the citation by contacting the police department parking administration manager in person, by 
telephone or mail within fifteen (15) days after the date the citation was issued, payment of court 
costs of ten dollars ($10.00), and requesting a hearing to contest the alleged violation. A request 
for a hearing will be considered received by the city if it is actually received by the police 
department parking administration manager within fifteen (15) calendar days after the date the 
citation was issued. A request for a hearing made by telephone will be considered received the day 

 
Page 3 of 3 
 
it is made if it is received by a police department employee between the hours of 8:00 a.m. and 
5:00 p.m. of any day, except on Saturday, Sunday or a city holiday. 
 
 
Section 3.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 4.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-07-02_TotalExtraction_55254713.pdf
 
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE CREATING SECTION 10.60.015, PAID ON-STREET 
PARKING DISTRICTS; ESTABLISHMENT; ADMINISTRATION, OF 
THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, 
ESTABLISHING THE PAID ON-STREET PARKING DISTRICTS 
PROGRAM 
AND 
MAKING 
CONFORMING 
AMENDMENTS 
AS 
SPECIFIED." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 10.60.015, Paid On-Street Parking Districts; Establishment; 
Administration, of Chapter 10.60, Parking Limitations, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby created to read: 
 
10.60.015 - Paid on-street parking districts; establishment; administration. 
 
A. There is created the paid on-street parking districts program, which shall be administered 
by the parking division within the Cheyenne police department in accordance with this 
section. The geographic area of any paid on-street parking district administered under this 
program shall be designated by resolution of the governing body. 
 
B. Within each paid on-street parking district as designated by the governing body, the 
parking administration manager for the division shall establish appropriate on-street 
parking zones and reasonable on-street parking rates. The purpose for establishing any 
parking zone or parking rate shall be to positively influence parking behavior, prioritizing 
customer and visitor parking while emphasizing off-street options for longer-term parkers, 
such business employees and city residents. When establishing any parking zone or any 
parking rate under this subsection, the parking administration manager shall take the 
following factors into consideration: 
 
1. The types of activities or businesses that to be encompassed within the proposed zone 
and whether the proposed parking rate will support the efficient use of the available on-
street parking;  
 
2. Whether dynamic parking management is appropriate for a proposed zone in order 
manage demand for on-street parking through variable parking rates and dissemination 
of real-time parking availability information; and 
 
3. Other parking conditions, such as availability and usage trends, for the purpose of 
tailoring the paid on-street parking districts program to better match the specific needs 
and dynamics of a parking zone and between zones to optimize parking management 
and utilization. 
 
3:17 pm, Jun 10, 2024

 
Page 2 of 4 
 
C. Before any parking zone or parking rate is established under subsection (D) of this section, 
and before any parking zone or parking rate may be modified by the parking administration 
manager, the division shall provide public notice of the proposed parking zone or parking 
rate, or modification thereof, by means of publication of the notice on not less than one (1) 
City of Cheyenne media platform. In addition, the notice required under this subsection 
shall be provided for a period of not less than thirty (30) days before the proposed parking 
zone or parking rate establishment or modification is set to take effect and shall seek to 
solicit public input on the proposal. 
 
D. Every parking zone established under this section shall include appropriate signage 
communicating the type of zone and the authorized payment methods as provided by the 
division. Authorized payment methods may include payments through electronic means 
including using webpages, smartphone applications or payment kiosks. The division may 
require all persons wanting to park a vehicle within a paid on-street parking district to first 
register for a user account for the purposes of securing on-street parking payments. 
 
E. Information regarding every parking district and parking zone, along with all attendant 
parking rates, shall be made publicly available by the division. The information required 
under this subsection shall include maps for any parking district and parking zone 
established under this section and shall be publicly accessible on a City of Cheyenne media 
platform. 
 
F. As used in this section: 
 
1. "Division" means the parking division within the Cheyenne police department. 
 
2. "Paid on-street parking district" or "parking district" means the geographical area 
specified by resolution of the governing body for the implementation of paid on-street 
parking as provided by this section; 
 
3. "On-street parking zone" or "parking zone" means the geographical area located within 
a parking district that is specified in accordance with this section, which exhibits 
consistent usage characteristics that allow for the assessment of parking patterns and 
determination of parking rates based on parking demand; and 
 
4. "On-street parking rate" or "parking rate" means the monetary charge levied by the City 
of Cheyenne for the privilege of parking a vehicle for a specified duration in a 
designated parking space within any paid on-street parking district.  
 
Section 2.  That Section 10.04.020, Definitions, of Chapter 10.04, General Provisions, 
Section 10.52.220, Parking Time Limited, of Chapter 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking 
Generally, and Section 10.60.010, Signed Time Limitations, of Chapter 10.60, Parking 
Limitations, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, are hereby 
amended to read: 
 
 

 
Page 3 of 4 
 
10.04.020 - Definitions. 
 
The following definitions apply. Whenever any words and phrases are not defined in this code 
but are defined in state laws regulating the operation of vehicles, such a definition controls.  
 
A. Except as otherwise provided, as used in this title: 
 
1. "Alley" means a street or highway intended to provide access to the rear or side of lots 
or buildings in urban districts and not intended for the purpose of through vehicular 
traffic;. 
 
2. "Bicycle" means every device propelled by human power upon which any person may 
ride, having two tandem wheels either of which is more than fourteen (14) inches in 
diameter;. 
 
3. "Central business (or traffic) district" means all streets and portions of streets within 
the geographic area bounded by Fifteenth 15th Street on the south, Twentieth 20th 
Street on the north, Reed Street Avenue on the west and Evans Street Avenue on the 
east;. 
 
4. "Curb loading zone" means a space adjacent to a curb reserved for the exclusive use of 
vehicles during the loading or unloading of passengers or materials;. 
 
5. "Freight curb loading zone" means a space adjacent to a curb for the exclusive use of 
vehicles during the loading or unloading of freight or passenger;. 
 
6. "Motor vehicle" or "vehicle" means as defined by W.S. 31-1-101(a)(xv); 
 
7. "Passenger curb loading zone" means a place adjacent to a curb reserved for the 
exclusive use of vehicles during the loading or unloading of passengers;. 
 
8. "School zones" mean the streets and alleys and immediate intersections adjacent to any 
property used for school and for school play purposes;. and 
 
9. "Traffic division" means the traffic division of the police department of the city or, in 
the event a traffic division is not established, then the police department of the city. 
 
10.52.220 - Parking time limited. 
 
When signs are erected in each block giving notice thereof, no person shall park a vehicle for 
longer than the time specified on posted signs on any day except Sundays and public holidays, 
unless otherwise indicated upon any of the streets within the city as designated by the city traffic 
engineer. Any person who has parked a vehicle in an area where parking is limited by time shall 
before the expiration of that time move the vehicle as required by Section 10.60.010 of the city 

 
Page 4 of 4 
 
code. The requirements imposed under this section shall not apply to paid on-street parking 
districts under Section 10.60.015 of the city code. 
 
10.60.010 - Signed time limitations. 
 
Vehicles parked Any person who has parked a vehicle in an area where parking is limited by 
time must shall move from the block face of the vehicle's parked position before the expiration of 
the time indicated on posted signs. This requirement applies on any day except Sundays and public 
holidays, or unless otherwise indicated upon any of the streets within the city as designated by the 
city traffic engineer. The requirements imposed under this section shall not apply to paid on-street 
parking districts under Section 10.60.015 of this chapter. As used in this section, "block face" 
means the one (1) side of a specific city block where signed time limited parking is available.  
 
"Block face" is defined as one side of a specific city block where signed time limited parking 
is available.  
 
Section 3.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 4.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

THIRD AND FINAL READING: ____________________________________

by the parking division within the Cheyenne police department in accordance with this 
section. The geographic area of any paid on-street parking district administered under this 
program shall be designated by resolution of the governing body. 
 
B. Within each paid on-street parking district as designated by the governing body, the

parking zone or parking rate shall be to positively influence parking behavior, prioritizing 
customer and visitor parking while emphasizing off-street options for longer-term parkers, 
such business employees and city residents. When establishing any parking zone or any

whether the proposed parking rate will support the efficient use of the available 
on-street parking;  
 
2. Whether dynamic parking management is appropriate for a proposed zone in order to 
manage demand for on-street parking through variable parking rates and dissemination 
of real-time parking availability information; and 
 
3. Other parking conditions, such as availability and usage trends, for the purpose of 
tailoring the paid on-street parking districts program to better match the specific needs 
and dynamics of a parking zone and between zones to optimize parking management 
and resource utilization.

C. Before any parking zone or parking rate is established under subsection (B) of this section,

D. Every parking zone established under this section shall include appropriate signage

10.16.080 - Citation on illegally parked vehicles. 
 
A. Whenever any motor vehicle without a driver is found parked, standing or stopped in 
violation of any of the restrictions imposed by ordinances of this city or by state law, the 
officer finding the vehicle shall take its registration number, vehicle identification number 
or any other information displayed on the vehicle which may identify its owner, and shall 
conspicuously affix to the vehicle a civil traffic citation. A Any violation of Section 
10.64.020 or under this section, Chapter(s) Chapters 10.52, 10.56, 10.60 and Section(s)

is unlawfully parked; 
 
3. Reference to the municipal ordinance or code provision alleged to have been violated; 
and

violation must be made and received by the police department parking 
administration manager within fifteen (15) days after the date the citation was

by the city traffic engineer. Any person who has parked a vehicle in an area where parking is 
limited by time shall before the expiration of that time move the vehicle as required by Section 
10.60.010 of the city code. The requirements imposed under this section shall not apply to paid 
on-street parking districts as provided by Section 10.60.015 of the city code. 
 
10.60.010 - Signed time limitations. 
 
Vehicles parked Any person who has parked a vehicle in an area where parking is limited by 
time must shall move from the block face of the vehicle's parked position before the expiration of 
the time indicated on posted signs. This requirement applies on any day except Sundays and public 
holidays, or unless otherwise indicated upon any of the streets within the city as designated by the 
city traffic engineer. The requirements imposed under this section shall not apply to paid on-street 
parking districts as provided by Section 10.60.015 of this chapter. As used in this section, "block 
face" means the one (1) side of a specific city block where signed time limited parking is available.  
 
"Block face" is defined as one side of a specific city block where signed time limited parking 
is available.  
 
10.60.040 - Fine schedule. 
 
 
Any such fine to be imposed pursuant to a notice of violation issued under Section 10.60.030 
of this chapter or a civil traffic citation under Section 10.16.080 of the city code, including any 
applicable administrative fees, shall be determined specified by resolution of the governing body, 
including any administrative fees, and shall be made available to the general public in the form of 
a fine schedule. Administrative fees shall be factored into the total amount of the fine before any 
late payment penalties are applied in accordance with Section 10.16.090(A) of the city code. 
 
A. Administrative fees shall be factored into the total amount of the fine before any 
late payment penalties are applied in accordance with Section 10.16.090 A. 
 
B. Court costs of ten dollars ($10.00) may be assessed against those requesting a 
hearing in accordance with Section 10.16.140. 
 

 
Page 6 of 6 
 
Section 3.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 4.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-07-02_TotalExtraction_74491363.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Seth Lloyd, AICP 
Subject: North Range Business Park Annexation    
Date: 
July 8, 2024 
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is a resolution to allow the City to proceed with a City-initiated annexation 
pursuant to Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402. North Range Business Park utilizes outside user agreements 
with BOPU to provide services. These agreements stipulate that the land owners agree to be annexed 
once contiguous with the City. With an annexation earlier in 2024, the City annexed land south of the 
park, making the business park contiguous to City limits for the purposes of state statute.  
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and referral  
July 8, 2024 
• 
Public Services Committee  
July 16, 2024 
• 
Final consideration 
 
July 22, 2024 
 
On March 29, 2024, City staff mailed a notice to the affected agencies pursuant to the Outside User 
Agreement dated May 5, 2005, and recorded at Book 1882, Page 18. 
 
According to state statute, the City may annex a property under 40 acers without landowner consent, as 
long as a majority of owners within the annexation do not protest the annexation. Any property over 40 
acres which pays property taxes and is not 100% surrounded by City limits requires written consent from 
the landowner. The properties meeting these requirements serviced by BOPU have given written 
consent in their Outside User Agreement. There is one adjacent property which does not currently have 
an Outside User Agreement which has been added to this annexation for convenience of the landowner, 
and that owner has given written consent to this annexation. 
 
Annexation also requires establishing a City zone. The current County zone is a PUD and requires 
consideration to properly modify into City zoning.    
 
Attachments: 
1. Resolution 
2. Map of properties 
3. Existing Outside User Agreements 
4. Written consent for property over 40 acres not subject to an Outside User Agreement  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION CERTIFYING COMPLIANCE WITH WYOMING 
STATUTE § 15-1-402 FOR THE CITY-INITIATED ANNEXATION OF 
TRACTS OF LAND SITUATE IN PORTIONS OF SECTIONS 4 AND 5, 
T13N, R67W, 6TH P.M., LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING (ALSO 
KNOWN 
AS 
'NORTH 
RANGE 
BUSINESS 
PARK', 
LOCATED 
NORTHWEST OF THE I-80 AND ROUNDTOP ROAD INTERCHANGE)." 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne desires to initiate annexation proceedings for a portion 
of Sections 4 and 5, T13N, R67W, Laramie County, Wyoming, said tract of land more particularly 
described as follows:  
 
Lot 3, Block 1, North Range Business Park; Lots 1-2, Block 6, North Range Business Park, 
3rd Filing; Lots 1-2, Block 8, North Range Business Park, 3rd Filing; Lots 1-5, Block 9, 
North Range Business Park, 3rd Filing; Lots 1-6, Block 10, North Range Business Park, 
3rd Filing; Lots 1-2, Block 11, North Range Business Park, 3rd Filing; Lot 2, Block 12, 
North Range Business Park, 4th Filing; Lot 3, Block 1, North Range Business Park, 5th 
Filing; Lots 1-2, Block 13, North Range Business Park, 6th Filing; Lots 2-4, Block 15, 
North Range Business Park, 8th Filing; Lot 1, Block 17, North Range Business Park, 10th 
Filing; Lot 1, Block 16, North Range Business Park, 11th Filing; Lot1, Block 18, North 
Range Business Park, 12th Filing; Lots 1-3, Block 1, North Range Business Park, 13th 
Filing; Lot 1, Block 1, North Range Substation; Lot 1, Block 1, BHE Ready Subdivision; 
 
AND 
 
The rights of way for Diamond Creek Drive, Veta Drive, Logistics Drive, McKinney Drive, 
and Horizon Drive as platted with North Range Business Park, 3rd Filing (noting that 
“Diamond Creek Drive” as shown on the plat was renamed “Bruns Way” on April 7, 2020, 
by action of the Board of County Commissioners), Laramie County, Wyoming.; 
 
WHEREAS, Wyoming Statute § 15-1-404 outlines the requirements of local governments 
in initiating annexation proceedings and requires that a resolution be adopted certifying 
compliance with Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402; 
 
WHEREAS, the proposed annexation is compliant with Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402. The 
proposed annexation meets the following criteria found in Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402(a):  
 
(i) The annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town;  
 
11:47 am, Jun 17 2024

(ii) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town;  
 
(iii) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or 
town and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the 
city or town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed;  
 
(iv) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or 
town, or the area meets the requirements of Wyoming Statute § 15-1-407;  
 
(v) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body 
is prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area 
pursuant to Wyoming Statute § 15-1-410; and 
 
(vi) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public 
hearing required by Wyoming Statute § 15-1-405(a), will send by certified mail to all 
landowners and affected public utilities within the territory a summary of the annexation 
report as required under subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and 
location of the public hearing required by Wyoming Statute § 15-1-405(a).  
 
WHEREAS, an annexation report has been created by the annexing City, containing, at a 
minimum, the required information from Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402(c).  
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne shall prepare for each landowner and affected public 
utility so requesting in writing, the estimated cost of infrastructure improvements required of the 
landowner and affected public utility related to the annexation pursuant to Wyoming Statute § 15-
1-402(e); 
 
WHEREAS, the annexation of this land does not create an area which is situated entirely 
within the boundaries of the City but is not annexed; and  
 
WHEREAS, all land meeting the criteria under Wyoming Statute § 15-1-412 requiring an 
owner’s written consent for annexation has had an owner give written consent to the annexation.  
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, THAT the land described above is compliant with Wyoming 
Statute §15-1-402, and that the procedures outlined in Wyoming Statute § 15-1-405 and Wyoming 
Statute § 15-1-406 shall hereby be initiated. 
 
 

PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor

paid by the DEVELOPER shall compensate the BOPU at the then 
current inspection fee charged to in-CITY users. 
4. 
Upon construction of said improvements and approval 
and acceptance thereof by the BOPU, 
the DEVELOPER shall 
transfer ownership of all sewer mains and manholes to the 
BOPU, together with all necessary easements and right-of-ways 
for the maintenance and operation of the same. 
The DEVELOPER 
shall warrant the improvements for a two year period from the 
date of transfer. 
USER ( s) 
shal 1 retain ownership of all 
improvements from the point of tapping into the sewer mains at 
the USER'S property line and shall be responsible for the 
maintenance, repair and upkeep thereof. 
5. 
The 
DEVELOPER shall comply with all applicable 
federal, 
state 
and 
local 
laws, 
rules, 
regulations 
and 
ordinances governing sanitary sewer systems including but not 
limited to Title 13 of the Cheyenne City Code. The DEVELOPER 
shall comply with all technical codes applicable to in-CITY 
construction which use or connect to the sewer mains including 
the 
BOPU 
standards, 
Uniform 
Plumbing 
Code, 
and Uniform 
Mechanical Code. 
6. 
The DEVELOPER and/or USER(S) shall pay 1.5 times the 
BOPU sewer rates presently in effect as adopted pursuant to 
Resolutions No. 3364 and 3365 for in-CITY users and shall be 
subject to all subsequent changes thereof. 
The DEVELOPER 
and/or 
USER(S) 
shall also pay 
a 
fifty dollar 
($50.00) 
administrative fee for each tap into a sewer main. 
In the 
event the BOPU negotiates a lower sewer rate with a similarly 
situated developer(s) and/or user(s), the BOPU, 
DEVELOPER 
and/or USER(S) agree to adjust the sewer rates in a manner 
consistent with that negotiated with the similarly situated 
developer(s) and/or user(s). 
In the event the BOPU negotiates 
a sewer agreement with a sewer district hereinafter created, 
the BOPU, DEVELOPER and/or USER(S) agree to adjust the sewer 
rates in a manner consistent with the sewer rates charged to 
the customers in said district by the sewer district; only if 
such sewer rate is lower than that which is set forth in the 
first sentence of this Paragraph 6. 
If annexation to the CITY 
takes place, the sewer rate and tap fees and other such 
charges will be not more than those charged to all other CITY 
users. 
If the DEVELOPER(S) and/ or USER(S) are not connected to 
CITY water and utilizing the same for domestic purposes, in 
order to compute monthly sewer charges, DEVELOPER(S) and/or 
USER(S) shall install a water meter on their domestic water 
supply pursuant to the following conditions: 
a) 
the BOPU shall furnish required meters which shall 
remain the property of the BOPU; 
b) 
the DEVELOPER(S) and/or USER(S) shall provide the 
necessary meter vault and plumbing such that a 
meter can be installed; 
c) 
the DEVELOPER(S) and/or USER(S) shall install the 
met.er; 
d) 
the BOPU shall maintain and read the meter from 
3

7. 
Monthly sewer charges shall be calculated based on 
actual sewer flow rates pursuant to the following conditions: 
a) 
Wal-Mart shall provide and install a sanitary sewer 
metering manhole and gravity flow meter with data 
recorder at a mutually agreed location. 
The flow 
meter shall be installed in accordance with the 
meter 
manufacture's 
recommendations 
and 
as 
inspected and approved by BOPU. 
b) 
the BOPU shall maintain and read the sanitary sewer 
flow meter from which monthly sewer charges shall 
be calculated. 
8. 
The DEVELOPER hereby consents to the annexation of 
the above-referenced property in the event said property 
qualifies for annexation to the CITY in accordance with WYO. 
STAT. 
§ 15-1-402 and Title 13 of the Cheyenne City Code as the 
same may be hereafter amended and, by warranty deed covenants, 
will require USER(S) to consent to the terms and conditions of 
this 
agreement 
including the 
annexation 
of 
the 
above-
referenced property which utilizes the sewer mains in the 
event said property qualifies for annexation to the CITY in 
accordance with WYO. 
STAT. 
15-1-4 02 and Title 13 of the 
Cheyenne City Code as the same may be hereafter amended. 
Before BOPU or the CITY commences any process or proceeding to 
annex the Property, BOPU and the City of Cheyenne will provide 
DEVELOPER and WAL-MART with prior written notice of their 
intent to do so. 
Such notice shall be provided to DEVELOPER 
and WAL-MART ninety (90) days prior to the commencement of 
such action. 
In the event such notice requires affirmative 
action by WAL-MART, and WAL-MART has not responded or replied, 
a second ninety (90) days prior written notice shall be given 
to WAL-MART. 
9. 
No 
septic systems will be 
allowed within the 
Subdivision so long as sanitary sewer service is furnished by 
the BOPU or its successors. 
10. 
DEVELOPER and USER(S) shall include the affirmative 
covenant attached hereto as Exhibit A and made a part hereof 
by this reference, with appropriate book and page numbers from 
the filing of this agreement with the Laramie County Clerk of 
Deeds, 
in all deeds from DEVELOPER and USER ( S) 
conveying 
property included in the above-referenced property and hereby 
assign an equal right to the CITY to enforce the same. 
11. 
The BOPU reserves the right to increase said sewer 
rate (to 2.0 times the in-CITY rate or the maximum allowed by 
law) to any property or lot within the Subdivision not annexed 
to the CITY if said property or lot qualifies for annexation 
to the CITY and the CITY requests such annexation; provided 
however, such right to increase said water rate shall not be 
exercised if the Subdivision is not annexed to the City in 
this instance through no fault of WAL-MART. 
12. 
The DEVELOPER and USER(S) shall not be entitled to 
and hereby waive any right to reimbursement from the BOPU and 
CITY for the cost of constructing said sanitary sewer system. 
The BOPU reserves all rights to use said sanitary sewer 
system as part of its entire sanitary sewer and sewage 
disposal system as may be necessary to provide service to

any 
damage 
occurs or action or proceeding

Cheyenne, Wyoming. See attached Exhibit D hereto.

This agreement made this 3.a_ day of Nov-s~~. 2016 between the City of 
Cheyenne, hereinafter referred to as the "CITY," a municipal corporation organized pursuant to the 
laws of the State of Wyoming, acting by and through its Board of Public Utilities, hereinafter 
referred to as the "BOPU," and Microsoft Corporation, hereinafter referred to as the "USER." 
WITNESS ETH: 
WHEREAS, the USER is the current owner of and.is desirous of connecting the property 
described as follows to the CITY water and sanitary sewer systems: 
Lot 3, Block 13, North Range Business Park Sixth Filing, 
according to the official plat filed for record in Laramie County, Wyoming; and 
WHEREAS, the BOPU has exclusive control of all CITY o;wned waterworks and sanitary 
sewer systems and operates the same for the CITY and may furnish surplus water and sanitary sewer 
facilities to persons outside the CITY pursuant to WYO. STAT.§ 15-7-404 (a) and Title 13 of the 
Cheyenne City Code. 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual promises and covenants herein 
contained, the parties agree as follows: 
1. 
The BOPU shall allow the USER to connect the above-referenced property to the 
existing BOPU water distribution system and sanitary sewer system at points determined by the 
BOPU. 
2. 
The BOPU shall provide all necessary inspections for the construction and installation 
of the service line connections. The USER shall provide the BOPU with a certificate of compliance, 
signed in affidavit form by a contractor licensed in the CITY, verifying that all construction materials 
and trade installation of service lines, service line fittings, and termination points at or in USER's 
buildings and at the location of the USER's buildings and at thelocation of the USER's water meter 
are in accordance with applicable provisions of federal, state and local codes, laws, and/or 
ordinances. Specific codes in effect include BOPU's standards, International Plumbing Codes and 
International Mechanical Codes. Specific reference shall be given stating that 1) there are no taps off 
of the service line between the water meter and the water main; 2) that a BOPU water meter has been 
installed; 3) a backflow prevention device has been installed; and ( 4) that the service lines are only 
servicing one building. Inspection fees to be paid by the USER shall compensate the BOPU at 1.5 
times the then current inspection fee charged to in-City users. The cost of the water meters installed 
will be paid for by the BOPU, not the users. 
3. 
The USER shall retain ownership of all improvements from the point of tapping into 
the water and sewer mains and shall be responsible for the maintenance, repair and upkeep thereof. 
The BOPU shall retain ownership of the water meter and shall be responsible for the maintenance, 
repair and upkeep thereof. 
4. 
The USER shall comply with all applicable federal, state and local laws, rules, 
regulations and ordinances governing sanitary sewer systems including but not limited to the 
applicable chapters of Cheyenne City Code Title 13. The USER shall comply with all technical 
codes applicable to in-City construction which use or connect to the sewer mains including the 
BOPU standards, International Phµnbing Codes and International Mechanical Codes.

5. 
The USER shall pay 1.5 times the BOPU water and sewer rates presently in effect for 
in-City users and shall be subject to all subsequent changes thereof. The USER shall pay 1.5 times 
the BOPU water and sewer tap fees, system development fees and all other applicable rates and fees. 
In the event the BOPU negotiates a different water and/or sewer rate with a similarly situated user, 
the BOPU and USER agree to adjust the water and/or sewer rates in a manner consistent with that 
negotiated with the similarly situated user. In the event the BOPU negotiates a water and/or sewer 
agreement with a district hereinafter created, the BOPU and USER agree to adjust the water and/or 
sewer rates in a manner consistent with the water and/ or sewer rates charged to the customers in said 
district by the water and/or sewer district. If annexation to the CITY takes place, the water and sewer 
rate and tap fees and other such charges will be not more than those charged to all other CITYusers. 
6. 
The USER hereby consents to the annexation of the above-referenced property in the 
event said property qualifies for annexation to the CITY in accordance with WYO. STAT. § 15-1-
402 and Cheyenne City Code Chapter 1.16 as the same may be hereafter amended and, by warranty 
deed covenants, will require future users to consent to the terms and conditions of this agreement 
including the annexation of the above-referenced property which utilizes the water and sewer mains 
in the event said property qualifies for annexation to the CITY in accordance with WYO. ST AT. 15-
1-402 and Cheyenne City Code Chapter 1.16 as the same may be hereafter amended. 
7. 
No drilling of water wells will be allowed within said property and no water wells 
shall be connected to the BOPU water system so long as water service is furnished by the BOPU or 
its successors. No septic systems will be allowed within said property so long as sanitary sewer 
service is furnished by the BOPU or its successors. 
8. 
The USER shall include the affirmative covenant attached hereto as Exhibit A and 
made a part hereof by this reference, with appropriate book and page numbers from the filing of this 
agreement with the Laramie County Clerk of Deeds, in all deeds from USER conveying property 
included in the above-referenced property and hereby assign an equal right to the CITY to enforce 
the same. 
9. 
The BOPU reserves the right to increase said water and sewer rate (to 2.0 times the 
in-CITY rate or the maximum allowed by law) to any property or lot within the above-referenced 
property not annexed to the CITY if said property or lot qualifies for annexation to the CITY and the 
CITY requests such annexation. 
10. 
The BOPU and CITY reserve all governmental/sovereign immunities as provided by 
law. The USER shall indemnify and hold harmless the BOPU and CITY, their officers, agents, 
employees and representatives from any claim or liability arising out of the failure to comply with 
the terms and conditions of this agreement or from any other action regarding the construction of 
said water distribution and sanitary sewer systems. 
11. 
If a provision, section, subsection, sentence, clause or phrase of this agreement is 
invalidated by any court of competent jurisdiction, such holding shall not affect the validity of the 
remainder of the agreement, which shall continue in full force and effect. 
12. 
The terms and conditions contained herein shall be a covenant running with the land 
and shall extend to and be binding upon the USER, its heirs, devisees, personal representatives, 
successors and assigns. 
13. 
This agreement contains all the agreements ofthe representations between the parties. 
None of the terms of this agreement shall be waived or modified to any extent, except by written 
instrument signed and delivered by both parties.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-07-02_TotalExtraction_48283691.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director  
Subject: A resolution expressing the support of the Governing Body for the submission of a petition 
by the Governing Body to the Downtown Development Authority for inclusion of property 
in the Downtown Development District. 
Date: 
July 8, 2024 
 
Background:  
 
This resolution authorizes the Mayor, or his designee, to petition the Downtown Development Authority 
Board to include recently acquired property into the Downtown Development District.  The property, 
recently referred to as the “Mead Triangle,” is a 0.70-acre parcel contiguous to the northern boundary 
of the Downtown Development District. The addition of the property to the Downtown Development 
District will allow other properties that become contiguous to seek inclusion in the district by similar 
petition, if so desired.   
 
The procedure for inclusion of additional land in the district is outlined in Wyoming Statute § 15-9-223 
which reads as follows: 
 
“15-9-223. Inclusion of additional property into development area; procedure. 
  
Subsequent to the organization of an authority, additional property may be included in the 
development area. Proceedings for inclusion shall be initiated by petition to the board of the 
authority signed by the owner or owners in fee of each parcel of land adjacent to the existing 
area sought to be included. The petition shall include evidence satisfactory to the board 
concerning title to the property and an accurate legal description thereof. If the board approves 
the application, it shall submit the application to the governing body of the municipality. If the 
governing body also approves the application, it shall then, at a regular or special meeting, by 
amendment to the ordinance treating the authority, redescribe the development area so as to 
include the additional property as described in the petition. From the effective date of the 
amendment the additional property shall be included within the development area and shall be 
subject to any taxes thereafter imposed by the municipality for the use and benefit of the 
authority.” 
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral 
July 8, 2024 
• 
Public Services Committee 
July 16, 2024 
• 
Final consideration 
 
July 22, 2024 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

The proposed expansion area is in the northwest portion of the northern part of the Downtown 
Development District and is shown below. In the event the Downtown Development Authority Board 
approves the addition of the property to the area, the request will be sent on the Governing Body for 
final consideration . Final consideration would be in the form of an ordinance that amends City 
municipal code to redescribe the Downtown Development District area.  Additional properties to the 
north and east may be included if accompanied by a similar petition.    
 
 
 
Inclusion of the property and other properties will extend the Downtown Development District’s 20.0 
mill tax levy. Municipal lands used for municipal use are exempt from the assessment. Ultimately these 
parcels will be used as to support the Reed Rail Corridor but may have interim City uses between now 
and then.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-07-02_TotalExtraction_75481015.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION SUPPORTING THE CLEAN CROW 
CREEK 
INITIATIVE ON BEHALF OF THE GOVERNING BODY FOR THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING." 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body for the city of Cheyenne, WY recognizes the need for 
clean water in our creeks to support a healthy community; 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body for the city of Cheyenne, Wyoming acknowledges the 
importance of protecting our creeks from stormwater pollution; 
 
WHEREAS, the Clean Crow Creek Initiative aims to improve water quality through the 
installation and maintenance of Wyoming made catch basin stormwater filters; 
 
WHEREAS, it is understood that in the past the city of Cheyenne has accepted stormwater 
filters donated by all the Rotary International Clubs of Cheyenne, the Boys and Girls Club, the 
Laramie County Conservation District, multiple anonymous donors, and Microsoft Corporation; 
 
WHEREAS, the city of Cheyenne encourages and will accept donations from sponsoring 
businesses, organizations, or citizens who wish to contribute to the community effort of protecting 
the creek; 
 
WHEREAS, the Clean Crow Creek Initiative will support the Public Works Department 
by reducing the amount of debris and pollutants entering the stormwater system, with the intent to 
decrease maintenance costs and improve operational efficiency; 
 
WHEREAS, the Clean Crow Creek Initiative aligns with the city's goals to protect the 
environment for the betterment of the community, enhancing the quality of life for residents by 
ensuring cleaner waterways, which can contribute to public health and recreational opportunities; 
 
WHEREAS, the implementation of the Clean Crow Creek Initiative helps the city comply 
with federal, state, and local environmental regulations, furthering the city’s efforts to meet or 
exceed standards that protect the creek from stormwater pollution, and recognizing that these 
efforts can contribute positively to the community; and 
 
WHEREAS, the city of Cheyenne is committed to exploring, accepting, and not 
unreasonably denying multiple methods of maintaining the stormwater filters, including city 
maintenance, hiring third-party vendors, or allowing third parties to clean the stormwater filters 
themselves with city approval (which will not be reasonably withheld). 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that staff of the city of Cheyenne will support the Clean Crow 
10:55 am, Jul 10 2024

Creek Initiative and its goals, including the installation and maintenance of stormwater filters to 
protect our waterways from pollution.

2024.
